# Licences

Every item here is CC0 1.0 (Creative Commons Zero) or public domain, as stated on its source page when it was retrieved. The `evidence/` folder holds a copy of each source page as it was read; the archive.org column links a public snapshot where one could be made.
Compositions by Bach, Satie, Grieg and Mendelssohn are in the public domain; the licence column is the recording's.

## Music added in v2

Release v2 adds these tracks; the v1 tracks above stay in release v1 and the manifest points at them there. Free Music Archive tracks were checked on their own page for the licence line, the “AI generated?” flag (only “No” taken) and “Instrumental”. Wikimedia Commons recordings count only when the recording itself is CC0 or released into the public domain (the page's licence templates are kept in the API record).

## Music added in v3

Release v3 adds these tracks; earlier tracks stay in their own releases (v1, v2) and the manifest points at them there. The v2 checks apply. In addition, a Wikimedia Commons recording published before 1926 counts as public domain in the US (17 U.S.C. 1401) when its page carries a recording template ({{PD-US-record-expired}}, {{PD-old-100-record-expired}}) and dates the recording itself; the year is given in the licence column. Each file was also run through Apple's sound classifier (no speech or singing).

## Music added in v5

Release v5 adds these tracks; earlier tracks stay in their own releases (v1–v3) and the manifest points at them there. Besides CC0 and public domain, this release takes music under Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY 4.0 or 3.0, as the source states): each such track names its author and carries the credit line in `licence.attribution` and in the table below. Only instrumentals of 60 s or more were taken: tracks whose source lists vocals or voice were skipped, and every file was run through Apple's sound classifier (no singing, speech or rap). Nothing marked or described as AI-generated.

| File | Title | Author | Source | Licence (as stated on the page) | Evidence | Retrieved | Processing |

## Music added in v6

Release v6 adds these tracks; earlier tracks stay in their own releases and the manifest points at them there. Besides CC0 and public domain, this release takes music under Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY 4.0 or 3.0, as the source states): each such track names its author and carries the credit line in `licence.attribution` and in the table below. Sources were read from their own bulk listings (incompetech.com's piece list, FreePD's category pages, Scott Buckley's library, Wikimedia Commons, OpenGameArt's licence-filtered search, the Free Music Archive's metadata dump joined to its live CC BY and public-domain listings), and each track's licence from its own page or record, saved under evidence/. Only instrumentals of 60 s to 8 min were taken: tracks whose listing names vocals were skipped, and every file was run through Apple's sound classifier (no singing, speech or rap). Nothing marked or described as AI-generated.

Freesound originals need an account to download, so the effects (and the Freesound music) were made from the high-quality preview MP3s each sound page offers publicly; the licence is the same.

Courtesy credits (not required by CC0 or public domain): Musopen and neolein ask to be credited when their recordings are used.

## Ambience (release ambience-v1)

Field recordings that AudioScape plays as ambience beds under narration, listed in `ambience.json`. Each was cut to a 60–300 s stretch, made into a seamless loop (the last 3 s crossfaded into the start), set to about −28 LUFS with true peak at or under −3 dBTP and encoded as AAC. Wikimedia Commons files count only when the recording itself is CC0 or public domain (the page's licence templates are in the API record).

## Ambience (release ambience-v2)

Field recordings that AudioScape plays as ambience beds under narration, listed in `ambience.json`. Each was cut to a 60–300 s stretch, made into a seamless loop (the last 3 s crossfaded into the start), set to about −28 LUFS with true peak at or under −3 dBTP and encoded as AAC. Wikimedia Commons files count only when the recording itself is CC0 or public domain (the page's licence templates are in the API record). Freesound recordings are CC0 or CC BY (3.0 or 4.0; no NC, SA or Sampling+); the audio is Freesound's 128 kbps preview of the upload, and for CC BY the Author column is the attribution to give (title, author, link, licence; the changes are in the Processing column). The API record and a snapshot of the sound's page are the evidence.
